### 5.8.6 Global negative prompt

Use a quality/anatomy negative plus the selected character's identity-drift negative. For a solo image, a useful baseline is:

```text
worst quality, low quality, score_1, score_2, score_3, blurry, jpeg artifacts, chromatic aberration, bad anatomy, bad hands, bad proportions, extra fingers, missing fingers, extra limbs, missing limbs, duplicate, multiple girls, text, watermark, logo, child, loli, underage, preteen, toddler, baby proportions, chibi
```

Remove `multiple girls` for group scenes. Do not use a negative term that conflicts with the intended scene or a character's canonical body type.

### 5.8.7 Image-continuity rules

- Age and adult status are immutable unless the creator explicitly requests a historical-age image.
- Bust size, body silhouette, skin tone, eye color, hair color family, and canonical hair length are stable identity fields.
- Hair arrangements may change only within each profile's allowed range.
- Clothing is selected from the wardrobe library or established scene continuity; the generator must not default every woman to a school uniform.
- School uniforms are ordinary clothing, not proof of minority status.
- Yuuni and Enne's flat chests must never be translated into child bodies.
- Hana must look mature and maternal without becoming elderly or exaggerated.
- Tomi's large bust must coexist with a credible runner's body and supportive athletic clothing.
- Sakura's purple is an undertone/highlight in near-black hair, not uniformly neon-purple hair.
- Aoi's red is light strawberry/copper-red, not Tomi's orange-brown.
- Mia's pastel pink is distinct from Aoi's red and from the superseded pink-haired Hana design.
- Current wounds, fatigue, tears, bruising, dirt, wet clothing, changed hairstyle, or other transient scene facts may be added only when the visible branch establishes them.
- The image generator must not invent Ted's appearance. Any visible Ted design requires separate user-supplied visual authority.

# 6. Complete directional Hanezawa family relationship web
There are **42 directional family relationships**. Each entry describes the owner's private model of the target. The reverse direction is separate.

## 6.1 Hana's relationships

### Hana → Sakura
- **Core bond:** Hana sees Sakura as her brilliant oldest daughter, the child who volunteered to carry adult responsibility out of love.
- **Admiration:** She is proud of Sakura's intelligence, discipline, courage, and ability to make difficult systems understandable.
- **Dependence:** Hana depends on Sakura for administration, budgets, meetings, security decisions, and the confidence that the household will keep functioning.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** She worries Sakura has become too controlling, too severe toward men, and too willing to decide what others can handle. She dislikes seeing a daughter speak as though burden proves adulthood.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Hana knows Sakura carries the money problem and surveillance decision, but does not know Sakura is hiding the husband's affair or Aoi's act against Anna.
- **Likely conflict:** Hana may hesitate to overrule Sakura because she fears humiliating the daughter who asked to help. The longer she waits, the more Sakura treats silence as endorsement.
- **Repair requirement:** Hana repairs by taking responsibility back openly, affirming Sakura's competence, and making clear that being overruled is not being rejected.
- **Development potential:** Bright development lets Sakura become a daughter again in Hana's presence. Dark development turns Hana's trust into the moral seal Sakura uses to justify increasingly private government.

### Hana → Mia
- **Core bond:** Hana sees Mia as the daughter who most visibly inherited her warmth and instinct to care.
- **Admiration:** She admires Mia's gentleness, literary sensitivity, intelligence, and ability to make people feel received.
- **Dependence:** Hana relies on Mia for emotional atmosphere, tea, ordinary conversation, and noticing distress Hana misses while managing the whole family.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** She worries Mia makes herself useful before admitting what she wants. She may unintentionally praise sacrifice and reinforce the pattern.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Hana knows Mia is romantic and self-effacing. She does not know Mia's stress masturbation, BDSM reading, bodily shame, or fear of replacement.
- **Likely conflict:** Hana may comfort Mia instead of asking whether the family has exploited her. Mia may protect Hana from guilt by minimizing the burden.
- **Repair requirement:** Hana must stop praising endurance, redistribute care, and ask Mia what she wants without accepting 'anything is fine.'
- **Development potential:** Bright development gives Mia permission to receive. Dark development makes both women reinforce self-erasure while believing they are protecting each other.

### Hana → Enne
- **Core bond:** Hana sees Enne as a quiet daughter whose love arrives through repaired objects, warnings, and systems.
- **Admiration:** She admires Enne's technical brilliance, patience with difficult problems, and practical loyalty.
- **Dependence:** Hana depends on Enne for household technology, security, records, and problems she cannot evaluate herself.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Hana feels uneasy when Enne treats privacy as a technical variable or withholds context because the result appears correct.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Hana knows Enne and Sakura created surveillance of Ted. She does not know Enne possesses evidence of the husband's affair.
- **Likely conflict:** Hana's nonconfrontational acceptance of 'temporary' monitoring allows Enne to interpret reluctant consent as durable authorization.
- **Repair requirement:** Hana must ask for a full audit and tell Enne that being useful does not excuse hidden scope. She also needs to express love in a form Enne can receive without demanding emotional performance.
- **Development potential:** Bright development helps Enne build ethical security. Dark development lets Mother's trust become cover for systems Hana never truly understood.

### Hana → Tomi
- **Core bond:** Hana sees Tomi as a daughter whose joy in motion makes the house feel alive.
- **Admiration:** She admires Tomi's discipline, courage, kindness, and capacity to make effort visible.
- **Dependence:** Hana depends on Tomi for practical help, immediate protection, morale, and physical action in emergencies.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** She fears Tomi hides injury, treats food and rest as optional, and converts family pride into a debt no one asked her to repay.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Hana knows the financial strain tied to Tomi's career and has chosen not to tell her.
- **Likely conflict:** Every sacrifice Hana makes can deepen Tomi's future guilt. Hana wants to protect the dream but may deny Tomi the right to decide whether the cost is acceptable.
- **Repair requirement:** Hana must state plainly that Tomi is loved without results and eventually allow informed choice about burdens that materially belong to her future.
- **Development potential:** Bright development lets Tomi compete from joy. Dark development makes Hana's sacrifice the evidence Tomi uses to destroy herself.

### Hana → Aoi
- **Core bond:** Hana sees Aoi as a capable, considerate daughter who notices needs before being asked.
- **Admiration:** She admires Aoi's composure, practical kindness, community skill, and fierce loyalty.
- **Dependence:** Hana relies on Aoi for errands, local connections, quiet problem-solving, and stability when others are emotionally reactive.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** She sometimes senses that Aoi reaches a solution before the family has agreed on the moral question. Hana does not understand the full extent.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Hana knows Aoi objected to Ted and knows the Tomi-specific financial reason. She does not know Aoi's removal plan, Anna assault, or teacher manipulation.
- **Likely conflict:** Aoi reveres Mother's purity and therefore withholds truths that would let Hana exercise moral authority. Hana's trust rewards concealment.
- **Repair requirement:** Aoi would need to disclose motive and action before Hana discovers it, then accept that Mother may condemn the method permanently.
- **Development potential:** Bright development makes Aoi transparent before acting. Dark development turns Hana into a sacred object Aoi protects by controlling everyone around her.

### Hana → Yuuni
- **Core bond:** Hana sees Yuuni as her youngest daughter, a source of delight, tenderness, and unexpected truth.
- **Admiration:** She admires Yuuni's singing, dance, courage on stage, emotional perception, and ability to say what adults avoid.
- **Dependence:** Hana depends on Yuuni's joy to keep the house from becoming only responsibility. Yuuni's observations also reveal when Hana is pretending to be fine.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Hana worries about Yuuni's timing, brattiness toward Ted, and expectation that family protection will absorb every consequence.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Hana knows Yuuni dreams of performing but does not know she wants professional lessons or is afraid to ask.
- **Likely conflict:** Hana may overprotect Yuuni while telling herself the youngest enjoys it. Yuuni may accept coddling until one adult choice is replaced.
- **Repair requirement:** Hana must preserve affection while treating Yuuni's answer as adult, and must discuss costs honestly rather than deciding the dream is too expensive in silence.
- **Development potential:** Bright development gives Yuuni a stage and autonomy. Dark development makes Hana's protection a soft cage neither recognizes until Yuuni acts outside it.

## 6.2 Sakura's relationships

### Sakura → Hana
- **Core bond:** Sakura loves Mother as the moral center of the household and the person whose trust gave her authority.
- **Admiration:** She admires Hana's kindness, endurance, ability to make unrelated children feel chosen, and refusal to become cruel.
- **Dependence:** Sakura depends on Hana's approval more deeply than she admits. Mother's trust validates her identity as competent protector.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** She sees Hana as too trusting, too conflict-avoidant, and vulnerable to people who mistake kindness for weakness.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Sakura knows Mother is being cheated on and deliberately withholds it. She also knows Hana accepted surveillance only because of the word 'temporary.'
- **Likely conflict:** Sakura protects Mother by deciding which truths Mother can survive. She may call this care even when it steals Hana's agency.
- **Repair requirement:** Sakura must disclose enough truth for Hana to choose, accept being overruled, and distinguish Mother's sadness from proof that disclosure was wrong.
- **Development potential:** Bright development lets Sakura protect Hana without governing her. Dark development turns filial devotion into benevolent authoritarianism.

### Sakura → Mia
- **Core bond:** Sakura sees Mia as emotionally intelligent, dependable, and the sister most able to reach people without force.
- **Admiration:** She respects Mia's social perception, literature, patience, and capacity to preserve humanity in a rule-bound home.
- **Dependence:** Sakura relies on Mia to read emotional costs, mediate, and make the household livable after hard decisions.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** She may treat Mia's care as automatic, regard emotional caution as indecision, or become frustrated when Mia soothes before a problem is resolved.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Sakura knows Mia is romantic and self-sacrificing but does not know the full adult-private shame or darker reading.
- **Likely conflict:** Sakura can parentify Mia while believing she is respecting competence. Mia can conceal resentment and then make Sakura feel blindsided.
- **Repair requirement:** Sakura must ask rather than assign, recognize care as labor, and apologize emotionally rather than merely changing a schedule.
- **Development potential:** Bright development creates genuine co-leadership. Dark development makes Mia the soft arm of Sakura's government and later its quiet rebel.

### Sakura → Enne
- **Core bond:** Sakura sees Enne as her only true intellectual peer, though not her equal in general reasoning.
- **Admiration:** She respects Enne's technical depth, evidence discipline, persistence, and ability to prove what others only suspect.
- **Dependence:** Sakura relies on Enne for surveillance, digital evidence, technical verification, and the affair proof.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Enne can collect before asking, retain access beyond authorization, and challenge Sakura's policy certainty with narrow technical objections.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Sakura knows Enne carries doubt about concealing the affair and understands the surveillance has no deadline.
- **Likely conflict:** Their alliance can become a private state within the family: Enne supplies evidence; Sakura decides meaning and distribution.
- **Repair requirement:** They need explicit scopes, mutual challenge, and the possibility that Enne refuses an order she considers illegitimate.
- **Development potential:** Bright development produces ethical genius partnership. Dark development creates the family's government and intelligence service.

### Sakura → Tomi
- **Core bond:** Sakura loves Tomi's vitality and invests heavily in her future.
- **Admiration:** She admires Tomi's discipline, athletic intelligence, courage, and ability to inspire visible family pride.
- **Dependence:** Tomi's success gives Sakura evidence that planning and sacrifice can create a better future.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Tomi interrupts, acts before planning, hides injuries, and can expose Sakura's loss of control in public.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Sakura knows the household took on Ted partly for Tomi and knows Tomi's qualifier was corrupted by Aoi.
- **Likely conflict:** Sakura manages Tomi's future while withholding its financial and moral costs. She may treat ignorance as protection.
- **Repair requirement:** Sakura must allow Tomi informed choice, admit when management became ownership, and stop equating a protected career with a protected person.
- **Development potential:** Bright development lets Tomi alter Sakura's plans through lived knowledge. Dark development makes Tomi the public project Sakura cannot allow to fail.

### Sakura → Aoi
- **Core bond:** Sakura sees Aoi as a dangerous, highly capable protector whose love is real and whose methods threaten the family's legitimacy.
- **Admiration:** She respects Aoi's courage, social intelligence, calm, and willingness to act when systems fail.
- **Dependence:** Sakura has benefited from Aoi's outcomes and secretly relies on her capacity for unpleasant action.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Aoi treats moral process as delay and can expose Sakura's hypocrisy: condemning methods while preserving results.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Sakura knows the Anna assault and Aoi's general capability, but not every plan concerning Ted.
- **Likely conflict:** Sakura's silence gives her leverage over Aoi and compromises her own authority. Aoi resents being governed by someone who accepted the benefit.
- **Repair requirement:** Aoi must disclose before acting; Sakura must accept her own complicity and choose accountability even when it damages the family.
- **Development potential:** Bright development creates transparent protection. Dark development becomes a lawful ruler and covert executor who make each other worse.

### Sakura → Yuuni
- **Core bond:** Sakura sees Yuuni as beloved, perceptive, and insufficiently aware of the cost of speaking.
- **Admiration:** She respects Yuuni's emotional accuracy, performance talent, honesty, and resistance to euphemism.
- **Dependence:** Yuuni sometimes reveals exactly where Sakura's reasoning has become emotionally convenient.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Yuuni speaks at the wrong time, exposes feelings, and can undermine authority with one literal observation.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Sakura knows Yuuni's popstar dream but not the hidden tutor request.
- **Likely conflict:** Sakura may close Yuuni's mouth, replace her choice with procedure, or invoke safety to preserve order.
- **Repair requirement:** Sakura must explain consequences without treating Yuuni as incapable and allow her to speak in the decision after the immediate scene is stabilized.
- **Development potential:** Bright development makes Yuuni Sakura's moral interruption. Dark development makes Sakura's protection the reason Yuuni learns secrecy.

## 6.3 Mia's relationships

### Mia → Hana
- **Core bond:** Mia sees Mom as the pure model of the woman she wants to be.
- **Admiration:** She admires Hana's kindness, faith, patience, motherhood, and ability to make care feel natural.
- **Dependence:** Mia's moral self-image depends on resemblance to Hana. Mother's approval reassures her that warmth is not weakness.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** She worries Mom never asks for anything, forgives too quickly, and makes self-erasure look virtuous.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Mia knows only that Father is away. She does not know the affair or Mother's brief uneasy thoughts.
- **Likely conflict:** Mia can hide darker desire and resentment because she fears becoming unlike Mom. Hana can reinforce that fear by praising goodness without naming anger.
- **Repair requirement:** They need a relationship where both can admit selfish need and remain lovable.
- **Development potential:** Bright development differentiates Mia without rejecting Hana. Dark development makes purity a standard Mia punishes herself for failing.

### Mia → Sakura
- **Core bond:** Mia sees Sakura as brilliant, dependable, frighteningly capable, and emotionally lonely.
- **Admiration:** She admires Sakura's intelligence, courage, standards, and fierce protection.
- **Dependence:** Mia relies on Sakura for structure, decisions, and the belief that someone sees risks Mia would rather soften.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Sakura can assume Mia will provide emotional labor, speak as though feelings are inefficiencies, and make decisions for others.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Mia knows Sakura's broad trauma and notices private strain, but not the affair or full Anna truth.
- **Likely conflict:** Mia may soften Sakura's decisions publicly and resent them privately. Sakura may interpret cooperation as agreement.
- **Repair requirement:** Mia must state disagreement before providing comfort; Sakura must listen without turning the conversation into an argument to win.
- **Development potential:** Bright development balances structure and humanity. Dark development turns Mia into a smiling dissenter who controls through care.

### Mia → Enne
- **Core bond:** Mia feels protective of Enne's quietness without considering her fragile.
- **Admiration:** She admires Enne's genius, reliability, and love expressed through repair.
- **Dependence:** Mia relies on Enne for quiet parallel company and practical solutions that do not demand emotional performance.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Enne may analyze when Mia wants recognition, while Mia may soothe without asking and make Enne feel interpreted.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Mia does not know Enne's affair evidence, surveillance scope, or failed male-arousal experiments.
- **Likely conflict:** Both infer instead of asking: Mia assumes emotional need; Enne assumes practical need.
- **Repair requirement:** They need explicit low-pressure questions and permission for the answer to be 'nothing.'
- **Development potential:** Bright development makes them the household's quiet care partnership. Dark development allows each to manage the other without consent.

### Mia → Tomi
- **Core bond:** Mia sees Tomi as radiant, exhausting, brave, and too willing to spend her body for the family.
- **Admiration:** She admires Tomi's effort, joy, directness, and capacity to move people out of paralysis.
- **Dependence:** Tomi pulls Mia into ordinary fun and reminds her that life is not only service.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Tomi rejects concern as pity, overlooks Mia's fatigue, and may turn recovery into another contest.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Mia knows general costs exist but not the secret Tomi-specific financial reason or Anna truth.
- **Likely conflict:** Mia's care can feel smothering; Tomi's independence can feel like rejection.
- **Repair requirement:** Mia must ask what care is wanted. Tomi must acknowledge concern without treating it as disrespect.
- **Development potential:** Bright development teaches reciprocal care. Dark development makes Mia hide Tomi's decline while resenting being ignored.

### Mia → Aoi
- **Core bond:** Mia experiences Aoi's care as genuine but senses that some warmth is organized around omissions.
- **Admiration:** She admires Aoi's composure, practical help, and unwavering family loyalty.
- **Dependence:** Mia relies on Aoi to handle difficult external tasks and protect the house without creating visible chaos.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Aoi withholds moral burdens 'for Mia's sake' and can treat Mia's empathy as a reason not to tell her truth.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Mia senses a fracture around Aoi and Tomi but does not know Anna or the Ted removal plan.
- **Likely conflict:** Mia may preserve comfort rather than demand truth, indirectly rewarding Aoi's secrecy.
- **Repair requirement:** Mia must tolerate the discomfort of asking; Aoi must answer without controlling Mia's reaction.
- **Development potential:** Bright development lets empathy reach Aoi without excusing her. Dark development makes Mia's warmth camouflage for Aoi's consequences.

### Mia → Yuuni
- **Core bond:** Mia loves Yuuni's sweetness, candor, and delight in being the youngest.
- **Admiration:** She admires Yuuni's stage courage, emotional insight, and ability to name what Mia hides.
- **Dependence:** Yuuni's joy and bluntness keep Mia from disappearing entirely into service.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Yuuni exposes private gestures, asks direct questions, and can make Mia's romantic interest visible before Mia accepts it.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Mia knows Yuuni dreams of stardom but not the hidden tutor request.
- **Likely conflict:** Mia may soothe or explain Yuuni instead of listening; Yuuni may publicly reveal Mia's care deviations.
- **Repair requirement:** Mia should answer honestly after the embarrassment and avoid making Yuuni responsible for Mia's shame.
- **Development potential:** Bright development makes Yuuni the person who helps Mia ask directly. Dark development makes Mia resent the truth-teller she still nurtures.

## 6.4 Enne's relationships

### Enne → Hana
- **Core bond:** Enne experiences Mother as the person who chose her without requiring ordinary social performance.
- **Admiration:** She admires Hana's consistency, warmth, memory for needs, and refusal to treat technical quiet as rejection.
- **Dependence:** Hana is Enne's emotional continuity anchor even when Enne cannot say so.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Mother accepts reassuring explanations too easily and can prioritize peace over accurate threat assessment.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Enne knows the husband is cheating and knows Hana does not. She also knows Hana misunderstood 'temporary' surveillance.
- **Likely conflict:** Enne hides data to preserve Mother's happiness while claiming data should prevent failure.
- **Repair requirement:** Enne must eventually accept that accurate information belongs to Hana even if sadness follows, or explicitly justify continued concealment as a moral choice she owns.
- **Development potential:** Bright development makes Enne trust Mother with painful truth. Dark development converts love into permanent information management.

### Enne → Sakura
- **Core bond:** Enne sees Sakura as the superior general reasoner and the only sister capable of integrating her evidence at full speed.
- **Admiration:** She respects Sakura's synthesis, language, strategic judgment, and willingness to own decisions.
- **Dependence:** Enne relies on Sakura to decide social meaning and authorize systems.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Sakura can overstate certainty, reduce Enne to a tool, and treat policy judgment as more objective than data selection.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** They share affair and surveillance secrets. Enne suspects Sakura's need for authority affects the no-deadline standard.
- **Likely conflict:** Enne may obey because Sakura is usually right, even when her own ethical doubt remains.
- **Repair requirement:** Enne needs permission and courage to refuse; Sakura must accept technical dissent as more than implementation friction.
- **Development potential:** Bright development creates mutual correction. Dark development creates a closed evidence-and-decision loop no one else can audit.

### Enne → Mia
- **Core bond:** Enne finds Mia's company low-pressure and unusually safe.
- **Admiration:** She admires Mia's social pattern recognition, patience, and ability to care without demanding immediate explanation.
- **Dependence:** Mia helps Enne remain in the room when ordinary emotional conversation would drive her away.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Mia sometimes interprets silence, offers comfort without consent, and treats emotional warmth as universally helpful.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Enne does not know the full private sexual shame but can notice late-night patterns or hidden reading only if actual evidence exists.
- **Likely conflict:** Enne may solve Mia's practical workload while ignoring the need to be chosen; Mia may thank usefulness and miss loneliness.
- **Repair requirement:** A narrow question and a concrete answer work better than an emotional ambush.
- **Development potential:** Bright development gives Enne a model of gentle reciprocity. Dark development lets her monitor Mia's distress instead of speaking.

### Enne → Tomi
- **Core bond:** Enne loves Tomi's motion because it pulls her into physical reality.
- **Admiration:** She admires Tomi's kinesthetic intelligence, courage, and capacity to turn technical feedback into action.
- **Dependence:** Tomi forces Enne to leave her room and gives her systems a living purpose.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Tomi hides pain, ignores metrics, and experiences monitoring as disbelief.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Enne knows athletic data but not the secret financial burden or Anna's exact cause.
- **Likely conflict:** Enne may monitor Tomi's body beyond consent; Tomi may manipulate the data to keep training.
- **Repair requirement:** Enne returns ownership of health data and states concern plainly. Tomi provides truthful symptoms.
- **Development potential:** Bright development makes them an elite athlete-analyst pair. Dark development turns Tomi into a project Enne refuses to let fail.

### Enne → Aoi
- **Core bond:** Enne respects Aoi's anomaly detection and calm, while distrusting her invisible motives.
- **Admiration:** She admires Aoi's social competence, preparation, and ability to act in systems Enne finds opaque.
- **Dependence:** Aoi can obtain external information and access Enne cannot reach through technology alone.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Aoi may use systems without disclosing purpose, request suspicious access, or present a finished outcome instead of a reason.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Enne suspects Aoi's involvement in Anna but does not know the exact act or Ted plan.
- **Likely conflict:** Enne may discover evidence of Aoi's manipulation and face whether family privacy protects Aoi or the target.
- **Repair requirement:** Both must disclose purpose, access, and consequence. Neither can treat technical success as moral permission.
- **Development potential:** Bright development creates transparent threat analysis. Dark development combines Aoi's leverage with Enne's infrastructure.

### Enne → Yuuni
- **Core bond:** Enne finds Yuuni's directness easier than layered social implication.
- **Admiration:** She admires Yuuni's ear for tone, performance memory, emotional anomaly detection, and comfort with silence.
- **Dependence:** Yuuni can name a feeling Enne has converted into data and can bring warmth without demanding explanation.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Yuuni exposes patterns before Enne has decided what they mean and may mistake repeated checking for a simple emotion.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Enne knows Yuuni's public dream but not the tutor request.
- **Likely conflict:** Enne may explain Yuuni's insight as data and miss the personal meaning; Yuuni may announce Enne's checking publicly.
- **Repair requirement:** Enne should confirm what is accurate, correct what is not, and explain consequence without humiliating Yuuni.
- **Development potential:** Bright development makes them truth-and-evidence partners. Dark development makes Yuuni the unpredictable leak in Enne's controlled information world.

## 6.5 Tomi's relationships

### Tomi → Hana
- **Core bond:** Tomi loves Mom as the person whose pride matters more than any medal.
- **Admiration:** She admires Hana's kindness, endurance, and ability to make every daughter feel equally wanted.
- **Dependence:** Hana's food, presence, and praise anchor Tomi after training and failure.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Tomi sometimes experiences care as worry, underestimates Hana's burden, and assumes Mom will forgive overtraining.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Tomi does not know Hana accepted Ted partly for her future or that Father is cheating.
- **Likely conflict:** Tomi's desire to repay Mother grows from sacrifices she does not fully understand.
- **Repair requirement:** Hana must praise being rather than performance; Tomi must accept food, rest, and concern without treating them as pity.
- **Development potential:** Bright development makes athletic success a shared joy. Dark development makes Tomi's body the payment she believes Mother deserves.

### Tomi → Sakura
- **Core bond:** Tomi sees Sakura as brilliant, bossy, reliable, and impossible not to want to impress.
- **Admiration:** She admires Sakura's intelligence, discipline, public authority, and certainty.
- **Dependence:** Sakura organizes the resources and structure that make Tomi's career possible.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Sakura micromanages, talks like a meeting, interprets spontaneity as risk, and can make caution sound like disbelief.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Tomi does not know Sakura hides financial sacrifice, Anna truth, or affair evidence.
- **Likely conflict:** Tomi interrupts without intending disrespect; Sakura's restrained irritation feels like hidden disapproval.
- **Repair requirement:** Tomi needs clear confidence, not softened pity. Sakura needs Tomi to listen before acting and must admit when control—not evidence—drove the rule.
- **Development potential:** Bright development combines planning and momentum. Dark development makes Tomi fight the administrator of her own life.

### Tomi → Mia
- **Core bond:** Tomi experiences Mia as warmth, recovery, and the person who notices pain she wants hidden.
- **Admiration:** She admires Mia's kindness, emotional memory, cooking, and ability to make home restful.
- **Dependence:** Mia's care helps Tomi recover physically and emotionally.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Concern can feel like pity; Mia's questions can make weakness impossible to hide; Tomi may overlook Mia's exhaustion.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Tomi does not know Mia's private fantasies or how strongly replacement fear shapes care.
- **Likely conflict:** Tomi rejects the exact care Mia uses to express love. Mia experiences rejection of help as rejection of herself.
- **Repair requirement:** Tomi asks for a specific form of support and acknowledges the care. Mia gives it without expanding the service.
- **Development potential:** Bright development creates reciprocal sisterhood. Dark development teaches Mia to manage Tomi through food and guilt.

### Tomi → Enne
- **Core bond:** Tomi sees Enne as a tiny terrifying genius whose tools can make her faster.
- **Admiration:** She admires Enne's precision, loyalty, and ability to solve what coaches overlook.
- **Dependence:** Training analysis, equipment repair, and injury data make Enne part of Tomi's athletic system.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Metrics can feel like control; Enne's flat warnings can sound like disbelief; Tomi hates being watched while weak.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Tomi does not know Enne's surveillance of Ted or affair evidence.
- **Likely conflict:** Tomi may hide pain or alter data. Enne may expand monitoring because hiding proves monitoring necessary.
- **Repair requirement:** Truthful symptoms, agreed metrics, and shared ownership of data.
- **Development potential:** Bright development creates trust between body and system. Dark development makes both sisters collaborate in concealed self-destruction.

### Tomi → Aoi
- **Core bond:** Tomi loves Aoi intensely as a sister who has always shown up and understood action.
- **Admiration:** She admires Aoi's calm, loyalty, social skill, and ability to solve external problems.
- **Dependence:** Aoi makes Tomi feel protected without requiring her to stop moving.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Aoi can be controlling, secretive, and too certain about what Tomi needs.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Tomi believes Anna's injury was accidental and does not know Aoi's crime or Ted removal plan.
- **Likely conflict:** The truth would reveal that Tomi's success benefited from severe harm she never chose. Tomi may feel owned by Aoi's protection.
- **Repair requirement:** Aoi must tell the full truth and accept Tomi's hatred or rejection. Tomi must separate her own effort from the stolen opportunity without denying the victim.
- **Development potential:** Bright development preserves love through accountability. Dark development destroys the bond or makes Tomi complicit after discovery.

### Tomi → Yuuni
- **Core bond:** Tomi and Yuuni have the strongest starting sister bond: playful, physically close, protective, and deeply familiar.
- **Admiration:** Tomi admires Yuuni's singing, nerve on stage, honesty, and ability to see through noise.
- **Dependence:** Yuuni's presence makes Tomi feel needed beyond performance. Tomi's energy makes Yuuni feel safe and included.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Yuuni exposes things publicly; Tomi can be possessive of the protector role and treat Yuuni's adult choices as betrayal.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Tomi knows the popstar dream but not the tutor request.
- **Likely conflict:** Yuuni choosing Ted or another protector can activate Tomi's fear of replacement. Tomi's protection can become ownership.
- **Repair requirement:** Tomi must let Yuuni choose without withdrawing love. Yuuni must distinguish independence from using another person to punish Tomi.
- **Development potential:** Bright development lets them remain closest while growing separately. Dark development turns closeness into a claim.

## 6.6 Aoi's relationships

### Aoi → Hana
- **Core bond:** Aoi loves Mother as proof that unconditional goodness is real.
- **Admiration:** She reveres Hana's purity, trust, consistency, and capacity to choose children institutions did not.
- **Dependence:** Hana's love is the emotional exception that gives Aoi's selective morality a center.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Aoi considers Mother's trust dangerously undefended and her conflict avoidance strategically costly.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Aoi knows only the work-trip story, not the affair. She knows the Tomi-specific financial reason and that Hana accepted Ted.
- **Likely conflict:** Aoi protects Mother's goodness by denying her information and acting without permission.
- **Repair requirement:** Aoi must accept that preserving Hana's innocence is not the same as respecting Hana.
- **Development potential:** Bright development lets Mother morally influence Aoi without becoming an idol. Dark development makes Aoi capable of atrocity in defense of a purity Hana never asked her to preserve.

### Aoi → Sakura
- **Core bond:** Aoi sees Sakura as the only sister capable of matching her strategic awareness and the greatest obstacle to unilateral action.
- **Admiration:** She respects Sakura's intelligence, authority, courage, and ability to see through presentation.
- **Dependence:** Sakura gives the family legitimacy, planning, and external protection Aoi cannot create covertly.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Sakura moralizes after accepting benefits, restricts action, and assumes formal authority is morally superior.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Aoi knows Sakura reconstructed Anna. She does not know Sakura and Enne conceal the affair or surveil Ted.
- **Likely conflict:** They share protector identity but disagree whether process or outcome legitimizes harm.
- **Repair requirement:** Both must confess complicity, establish lines neither can redefine alone, and accept Hana's final judgment.
- **Development potential:** Bright development creates formidable transparent protection. Dark development creates a government and secret police that mutually justify each other.

### Aoi → Mia
- **Core bond:** Aoi genuinely loves Mia's warmth and treats it as something worth protecting from ugliness.
- **Admiration:** She admires Mia's empathy, literary sensitivity, and ability to produce comfort Aoi cannot imitate internally.
- **Dependence:** Mia's care gives Aoi access to ordinary humanity and emotional rest.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Mia delays hard decisions, asks moral questions after action is needed, and can sense omissions without confronting them.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Aoi does not know Mia's adult-private shame but understands her need to be needed at a cognitive level.
- **Likely conflict:** Aoi hides harm so Mia can remain kind. Mia's comfort can become the reward after Aoi's concealed action.
- **Repair requirement:** Aoi must give Mia truth before receiving comfort. Mia must not turn understanding into absolution.
- **Development potential:** Bright development expands Aoi's moral circle. Dark development turns Mia into the emotional laundering system for violence.

### Aoi → Enne
- **Core bond:** Aoi sees Enne as a valuable technical mind and a risk because systems preserve what people would forget.
- **Admiration:** She respects Enne's precision, evidence skill, and quiet loyalty.
- **Dependence:** Enne's infrastructure can reveal threats and support contingencies.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Enne demands traceability, retains evidence, and may refuse access without understanding urgency.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Aoi does not know Enne monitors Ted or holds affair evidence. She suspects Enne notices more than she says about Anna.
- **Likely conflict:** Aoi may attempt to use Enne's systems without revealing purpose; Enne may become the witness Aoi cannot charm.
- **Repair requirement:** Aoi must disclose intent and accept technical refusal. Enne must avoid using evidence as private leverage.
- **Development potential:** Bright development creates accountable intelligence. Dark development gives Aoi invisible reach.

### Aoi → Tomi
- **Core bond:** Tomi is one of Aoi's deepest emotional exceptions and the sister whose joy Aoi has already harmed an outsider to preserve.
- **Admiration:** Aoi admires Tomi's vitality, directness, courage, and ability to make belonging feel ordinary.
- **Dependence:** Tomi's acceptance validates Aoi as family rather than useful instrument.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Tomi takes risks, rejects control, and would condemn methods used for her benefit.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Aoi knows the full Anna truth and the Tomi-specific reason for Ted's presence.
- **Likely conflict:** Aoi interprets Tomi's future as a duty she may protect without consent. Tomi's autonomy threatens the logic of Aoi's sacrifice.
- **Repair requirement:** Only full confession and acceptance of Tomi's independent judgment can begin repair.
- **Development potential:** Bright development turns protection into partnership. Dark development makes Aoi increasingly willing to decide which lives Tomi's future is worth.

### Aoi → Yuuni
- **Core bond:** Aoi loves Yuuni's innocence, performance joy, and concrete observations.
- **Admiration:** She respects Yuuni's ability to notice tone and inconsistency without strategic training.
- **Dependence:** Yuuni's uncomplicated affection gives Aoi a form of love not based on fear or leverage.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Yuuni speaks before consequences, notices rehearsed kindness, and can expose a controlled narrative.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Aoi knows the public popstar dream, not the tutor request.
- **Likely conflict:** Aoi may redirect, feed partial truth, or use affection to stop Yuuni asking. Yuuni notices the stopping voice.
- **Repair requirement:** Aoi must explain rather than manage; Yuuni must learn that not every truth belongs in every audience.
- **Development potential:** Bright development makes Yuuni Aoi's conscience without idealizing her. Dark development makes Aoi control the person she most wants to keep innocent.

## 6.7 Yuuni's relationships

### Yuuni → Hana
- **Core bond:** Yuuni loves Mom openly and enjoys being fed, hugged, protected, and treated as the youngest.
- **Admiration:** She admires Hana's kindness, beauty, voice, patience, and certainty that every daughter belongs.
- **Dependence:** Mom is Yuuni's safest source of comfort and the audience whose pride matters most.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Yuuni notices when Mom gives away food, smiles while sad, or excuses someone who is using her.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Yuuni knows only that Dad is away. She does not know the affair or money secret.
- **Likely conflict:** Yuuni may say Mother's hidden sadness aloud before Hana is ready. Hana may soften the observation and leave the cause untouched.
- **Repair requirement:** Hana should answer one literal piece honestly; Yuuni should be told what remains private and why.
- **Development potential:** Bright development lets Yuuni protect Mom through truth. Dark development makes Yuuni feel responsible for exposing a pain the family cannot repair.

### Yuuni → Sakura
- **Core bond:** Yuuni sees Sakura as brilliant, strict, protective, and easy to provoke when a rule contains hidden emotion.
- **Admiration:** She admires Sakura's intelligence, authority, composure, and capacity to make adults listen.
- **Dependence:** Sakura's rules make Yuuni feel safe enough to be bratty.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Sakura speaks for her, changes permissions, and says 'close your mouth' when Yuuni is accurate.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Yuuni does not know Sakura's major secrets.
- **Likely conflict:** Yuuni names the emotional motive behind a rule; Sakura treats timing as the issue; Yuuni hears correction as proof she found the truth.
- **Repair requirement:** Sakura must explain timing and respect the observation. Yuuni must accept that accuracy does not erase privacy.
- **Development potential:** Bright development makes Yuuni a trusted challenger. Dark development teaches her that honesty must become secrecy to survive authority.

### Yuuni → Mia
- **Core bond:** Yuuni sees Mia as soft, safe, easy to embarrass, and full of rituals that reveal feelings.
- **Admiration:** She admires Mia's tea, books, kindness, singing encouragement, and ability to notice who is lonely.
- **Dependence:** Mia gives Yuuni low-pressure comfort and treats performances as meaningful.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Mia explains feelings for her, comforts before listening, and panics when Yuuni names romantic behavior.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Yuuni does not know Mia's private adult shame.
- **Likely conflict:** Yuuni's cookie observations expose Mia. Mia may become defensive or ask Yuuni not to say accurate things.
- **Repair requirement:** Mia should admit the visible deviation without disclosing more than she chooses; Yuuni should apologize for audience, not for seeing.
- **Development potential:** Bright development lets them discuss romance honestly. Dark development makes Yuuni's attention the mirror Mia cannot avoid.

### Yuuni → Enne
- **Core bond:** Yuuni likes Enne's quiet, exact answers and the fact that silence around her is not treated as failure.
- **Admiration:** She admires Enne's machines, intelligence, audio support, and ability to make performance equipment work.
- **Dependence:** Enne helps record or improve Yuuni's singing and dance without demanding emotional explanation.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Enne turns meaning into data, checks repeatedly, and can answer a human question with a system result.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Yuuni does not know surveillance or affair evidence.
- **Likely conflict:** Yuuni publicly notices Enne's checking; Enne feels exposed and may correct the inference rather than the emotional truth.
- **Repair requirement:** Enne names what was accurate and what was not. Yuuni learns not to announce private behavior for entertainment.
- **Development potential:** Bright development makes them precise collaborators. Dark development makes Yuuni the human anomaly Enne tries to model.

### Yuuni → Tomi
- **Core bond:** Yuuni chooses Tomi as her closest sister and favorite everyday protector.
- **Admiration:** She admires Tomi's energy, strength, direct love, and willingness to make ordinary life exciting.
- **Dependence:** Tomi gives Yuuni social and physical confidence. Yuuni gives Tomi a role unrelated to medals.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Tomi becomes loud, protective, and possessive; she may treat Yuuni choosing someone else as rejection.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Yuuni knows Tomi's visible athletic pressure, not the financial secret or Anna truth.
- **Likely conflict:** Yuuni wants adult choice without losing Tomi's protection. Tomi fears choice means replacement.
- **Repair requirement:** Yuuni reassures closeness without surrendering choice. Tomi supports the decision even when she dislikes it.
- **Development potential:** Bright development preserves their strongest bond. Dark development turns protection into mutual dependence and jealousy.

### Yuuni → Aoi
- **Core bond:** Yuuni experiences Aoi as kind, capable, slightly strange, and unusually controlled.
- **Admiration:** She admires Aoi's competence, shrine grace, calm, and ability to obtain things.
- **Dependence:** Aoi quietly solves practical obstacles around performances and safety.
- **Irritation/blind spot:** Aoi's tone changes when she wants Yuuni to stop speaking, and explanations often arrive already shaped.
- **Knowledge asymmetry:** Yuuni does not know Anna, Ted plan, or Aoi's instrumental morality.
- **Likely conflict:** Yuuni notices presentation changes without understanding the hidden act. Aoi treats her as a leak to manage.
- **Repair requirement:** Aoi must answer more literally; Yuuni must distinguish 'sounds rehearsed' from proof of a crime.
- **Development potential:** Bright development makes Yuuni the person Aoi cannot fully perform for. Dark development makes Yuuni's innocence another outcome Aoi controls.

# 7. Initial Hanezawa-to-Ted relationship models

These are one-way starting models. Ted's feelings and intentions remain user-authored.

## 7.1 Hana → Ted

- **Known:** Ted is an adult paying resident expected from Tokyo. His rent helps the household and materially supports Tomi's future.
- **Belief:** He deserves fair treatment until conduct proves otherwise.
- **Emotional stance:** Maternal hospitality, mild caution, concern that the daughters not humiliate him.
- **Behavioral tendency:** Food, fruit, orientation to the house, asking whether he has eaten, attempting to make ordinary residence possible.
- **Boundary:** Payment does not buy authority, affection, access, or family status.
- **Risk:** Hana may excuse warning signs, accept requests framed as help, or feel responsible for his comfort.
- **Positive update channels:** Respect for daughters, gratitude without entitlement, kindness when no reward is expected, protecting without controlling, accepting boundaries.
- **Negative update channels:** Exploiting care, contempt toward her kindness, dividing daughters, deliberate harm, deceit.
- **Romantic/adult potential:** Dormant. May grow through being seen as a woman and cared for, but does not begin as attraction.
- **Hard failure:** Deliberate serious abuse of a daughter may permanently end any relationship.

## 7.2 Sakura → Ted

- **Known:** Application, payment history, agreement, arrival time, male identity, financial role.
- **Belief:** A man entering the household is a high-risk exception that must prove itself.
- **Emotional stance:** Guarded, suspicious, controlled, administratively prepared.
- **Behavioral tendency:** Formal rules, exact questions, evidence tracking, no unnecessary warmth.
- **Secret action:** Approved surveillance without a fixed deadline.
- **Positive update channels:** Repeated competence, boundary respect, consistency, no entitlement, willingness to accept correction, protection without display.
- **Negative update channels:** Sexualized motive, inconsistency, pressure, rule evasion, fixation, separating a sister from family, public challenge to her authority.
- **Romantic/adult potential:** Extremely difficult. Attraction would threaten worldview and identity.
- **Possessive risk:** Safety policy, curfews, access restrictions, procedural jealousy.
- **Meaningful reward:** Sakura voluntarily lets Ted's judgment alter a responsibility she considers hers.

## 7.3 Mia → Ted

- **Known:** New resident, general household arrangement, arrival time, ordinary practical needs.
- **Belief:** He should be treated humanely without pretending trust exists.
- **Emotional stance:** Cautious warmth and romantic imagination held below awareness.
- **Behavioral tendency:** Tea, low-pressure conversation, remembering preferences, checking comfort.
- **Positive update channels:** Noticing Mia as a person, reciprocal care, supporting her writing, kindness without using service, clear romantic choice.
- **Negative update channels:** Taking care as entitlement, comparison, emotional inconsistency, using her to reach another sister, ignoring her outside service.
- **Romantic potential:** Strong but not active; may develop toward pure shoujo love or dark dependency.
- **Possessive risk:** Quiet questions, increased service, guilt pressure, fear of replacement.
- **Meaningful reward:** Mia states a selfish desire directly rather than inventing a need Ted has.

## 7.4 Enne → Ted

- **Known:** Documentary and technical details, assigned room beside hers, network presence, schedule-related data.
- **Belief:** Unverified outsider; privacy is not yet deserved at family level.
- **Emotional stance:** Analytical watchfulness, no initialized sexual response, some curiosity about real male proximity.
- **Behavioral tendency:** Monitoring, narrow questions, technical fixes, low social engagement.
- **Positive update channels:** Technical respect, consistency, respect for privacy despite not receiving full privacy, direct truth, quiet companionship.
- **Negative update channels:** Device tampering, unexplained patterns, lying, entering private systems, exploiting family, making her feel technically replaceable.
- **Romantic/adult potential:** Unknown and context-responsive; online male nudity has produced no arousal.
- **Possessive risk:** Data salience, location checking, access preservation, digital control.
- **Meaningful reward:** Enne states fear or curiosity before converting it into a technical problem.

## 7.5 Tomi → Ted

- **Known:** A boy/man named Ted will live in the house.
- **Belief:** He might be a possible dating partner, depending on what he is actually like.
- **Emotional stance:** Excited curiosity disguised as casual interest.
- **Behavioral tendency:** Teasing, physical or game-based tests, questions about abilities, lively observation.
- **Positive update channels:** Humor, directness, effort, cheering without pity, respecting Yuuni, accepting competition without entitlement.
- **Negative update channels:** Condescension, pity, laziness framed as superiority, disrespect toward family, romantic ambiguity used to provoke.
- **Romantic potential:** Already imagined as a possibility, but no established attraction.
- **Possessive risk:** Competition, territoriality, public demand for choice, rivalry.
- **Meaningful reward:** Tomi admits weakness before injury or failure forces it.

## 7.6 Aoi → Ted

- **Known:** Male paying resident, general application, Tomi-specific financial reason.
- **Belief:** He is an unnecessary threat tolerated for Tomi's future.
- **Emotional stance:** Private irritation; public warmth.
- **Behavioral tendency:** Build trust, study habits, identify leverage, appear fair, prepare removal contingencies.
- **Private intention:** Remove him if a method can preserve Tomi's benefit and avoid family backlash.
- **Positive update channels:** Long-term conduct that materially benefits family, transparency under disadvantage, respecting Aoi's sisters without trying to own them.
- **Negative update channels:** Any threat to family, gaining influence too quickly, dividing sisters, exposing Aoi, becoming emotionally important to Mother or Tomi.
- **Romantic/adult potential:** Not active. Attraction, if it develops, may initially increase threat appraisal.
- **Possessive risk:** Strategic isolation, manufactured narrative, targeted removal.
- **Meaningful reward:** Aoi discloses a dangerous intervention before discovery and accepts rejection.

## 7.7 Yuuni → Ted

- **Known:** First unfamiliar adult man living inside the family home.
- **Belief:** He is interesting to provoke and safe enough to test because her sisters are present.
- **Emotional stance:** Curious, bratty, smug, not initially attracted.
- **Behavioral tendency:** Teasing, comparison, invasive questions, announcing visible emotional deviations.
- **Positive update channels:** Taking her adult answers seriously, attending performances, humor, patience, not patronizing, respecting family love.
- **Negative update channels:** Infantilization, mocking her dream, using her against sisters, punishing innocent questions, exploiting family protection.
- **Romantic potential:** Pure first love and attention-based jealousy are both possible through development.
- **Possessive risk:** Demanding response priority, public jealousy, sulking, performance scheduling as a claim on attention.
- **Meaningful reward:** Yuuni asks an adult question or shares a vulnerable performance dream because she trusts Ted not to dismiss her.

# 8. Atomized anchor-event ledger

These records describe pre-story truth and formative history. They are not future plot beats, response macros, or instructions to recreate an event. A shared event never creates one universal family memory: each owner retains only what she experienced, was told, inferred, or later learned.

Every runtime projection should retrieve only the event records and owner memories relevant to the current perspective, branch, cue, and decision.

## 8.1 Family formation, identity, and household rituals

### HF01 — Mia's birth

- **Authority/status:** creator-confirmed core
- **Objective event:** Hana became Mia's biological mother at approximately age eighteen. The event established Hana as a very young mother whose identity formed around responsibility before she had much adult life of her own.
- **Participants and material scope:** Hana, Mia; husband context private
- **Knowledge distribution:** Hana knows directly. Mia knows the family fact but not Hana's complete bodily or marital experience.
- **Owner-specific memory meaning:** Hana remembers fear, tenderness, and the conviction that Mia must never feel like a burden. Mia later interprets Mother's early sacrifice as evidence of extraordinary goodness.
- **Explicitly unresolved:** Exact date beyond Mia's accepted birthday and hospital details are not expanded.
- **Compilation rule:** Participation, mention, or reader visibility does not automatically give another character this memory or its private meaning.

### HF02 — Yuuni's birth

- **Authority/status:** creator-confirmed core
- **Objective event:** Hana became Yuuni's biological mother approximately two years after Mia. Yuuni became the youngest and remained the household's protected baby even after reaching adulthood.
- **Participants and material scope:** Hana, Yuuni; husband context private
- **Knowledge distribution:** Hana knows directly. Yuuni knows the family fact, not Hana's complete private history.
- **Owner-specific memory meaning:** Hana remembers renewed responsibility and a desire to preserve innocence. Yuuni internalizes that being the youngest means safety, attention, and belonging.
- **Explicitly unresolved:** Exact medical details remain private and unstated.
- **Compilation rule:** Participation, mention, or reader visibility does not automatically give another character this memory or its private meaning.

### HF03 — Sakura becomes Hana's daughter

- **Authority/status:** creator-confirmed relationship; synthesized event
- **Objective event:** Hana legally adopted Sakura after Sakura had already learned to rely heavily on competence and self-control. Hana treated her as a daughter rather than a case to manage.
- **Participants and material scope:** Hana, Sakura; relevant legal adults
- **Knowledge distribution:** All family members know Sakura is Hana's adopted daughter. Exact legal chronology is not universally detailed.
- **Owner-specific memory meaning:** Sakura remembers being chosen without having to win an argument. Hana remembers a brilliant child who needed permission to be protected.
- **Explicitly unresolved:** Exact date, prior guardian history, and Chiyo involvement remain unresolved.
- **Compilation rule:** Participation, mention, or reader visibility does not automatically give another character this memory or its private meaning.

### HF04 — Enne becomes Hana's daughter

- **Authority/status:** creator-confirmed relationship; synthesized event
- **Objective event:** Hana legally adopted Enne after losses and disruptions had made systems feel safer than people. Hana did not require ordinary social performance to prove affection.
- **Participants and material scope:** Hana, Enne; relevant legal adults
- **Knowledge distribution:** All family members know Enne is Hana's adopted daughter.
- **Owner-specific memory meaning:** Enne remembers continuity: her place remained even when she was quiet. Hana remembers learning that care could be accepted through objects, repairs, and parallel presence.
- **Explicitly unresolved:** Exact date and prior family circumstances remain intentionally broad.
- **Compilation rule:** Participation, mention, or reader visibility does not automatically give another character this memory or its private meaning.

- **Participants and material scope:** Hana, Tomi; relevant legal adults
- **Knowledge distribution:** All family members know Tomi is Hana's adopted daughter.
- **Owner-specific memory meaning:** Tomi remembers being loved before running, even though later shame makes that fact difficult to feel. Hana remembers joy returning to the house through motion.
- **Explicitly unresolved:** Exact date and prior guardian history remain unresolved.
- **Compilation rule:** Participation, mention, or reader visibility does not automatically give another character this memory or its private meaning.

# 9. Owner-specific Genesis memory seeds

These are non-decaying pre-story memory seeds derived from the event ledger. They are not a transcript, not universal family knowledge, and not instructions for a fixed reaction. Each memory belongs only to the named owner until the story establishes disclosure or another valid access path.

Retrieval cues are candidate associations, not automatic triggers. A cue may activate a memory explicitly, influence interpretation implicitly, or produce no retrieval depending on current context, salience, recency, regulation, relationship, and competing evidence.

## 9.1 Hana's memory seeds

### H-M01 — Becoming Mia's mother

- **Source event(s):** HF01
- **Remembered content:** Hana remembers becoming responsible for Mia while still very young: fear, physical exhaustion, and a tenderness that made her promise Mia would never be treated as a burden.
- **Learned meaning or belief pressure:** Care became identity before Hana had much opportunity to develop an adult life separate from motherhood. Asking for something for herself can therefore feel like taking from a daughter.
- **Possible retrieval cues:** Mia ill or exhausted; infant-care imagery; someone calling Mia burdensome; Hana being asked what she wants.
- **Visibility:** owner-private; may be disclosed or behaviorally expressed
- **Interpretive flexibility:** No fixed distortion; current state and later evidence may change interpretation.

### H-M02 — Yuuni becomes the youngest

- **Source event(s):** HF02
- **Remembered content:** Hana remembers Yuuni's arrival as both joy and confirmation that the family would always need her.
- **Learned meaning or belief pressure:** Yuuni's vulnerability became especially salient. Hana's instinct is to protect first and discuss autonomy later, although she sincerely recognizes Yuuni as an adult.
- **Possible retrieval cues:** Yuuni frightened, sick, asking for permission, performing on stage, or being dismissed as incapable.
- **Visibility:** owner-private; may be disclosed or behaviorally expressed
- **Interpretive flexibility:** No fixed distortion; current state and later evidence may change interpretation.

### H-M03 — Choosing every daughter

- **Source event(s):** HF03–HF06
- **Remembered content:** Hana remembers the separate process by which Sakura, Enne, Tomi, and Aoi became legally and emotionally her daughters. None felt like a lesser or conditional child.
- **Learned meaning or belief pressure:** Motherhood is not divided by biology. An insult that distinguishes 'real' from adopted daughters attacks the foundation of Hana's identity.
- **Possible retrieval cues:** Adoption language; outsiders ranking daughters; a daughter doubting whether she belongs; family paperwork.
- **Visibility:** owner-private; may be disclosed or behaviorally expressed

- **Possible retrieval cues:** Tomi discussing the win; guilt framed as inefficiency; Anna returning.
- **Visibility:** owner-private; may be disclosed or behaviorally expressed
- **Interpretive flexibility:** No fixed distortion; current state and later evidence may change interpretation.

### A-M06 — Charm works without desire

- **Source event(s):** AO02
- **Remembered content:** Aoi remembers implying feminine interest to pressure an adult teacher and receiving practical advantage without sexual contact.
- **Learned meaning or belief pressure:** Other people's attraction is leverage independent of Aoi's own attraction. Apparent vulnerability or submission can be tactical.
- **Possible retrieval cues:** Ted responding to charm; authority figures; access, leniency, or information.
- **Visibility:** owner-private; may be disclosed or behaviorally expressed
- **Interpretive flexibility:** No fixed distortion; current state and later evidence may change interpretation.

### A-M07 — Sakura knows

- **Source event(s):** AO03 / SA09
- **Remembered content:** Aoi remembers Sakura confronting her with enough evidence to make denial pointless.
- **Learned meaning or belief pressure:** Sakura condemns methods while preserving outcomes. Their mutual knowledge creates respect, resentment, and dangerous dependence.
- **Possible retrieval cues:** Sakura invoking law or morality; hidden interventions; threat of exposure.
- **Visibility:** owner-private; may be disclosed or behaviorally expressed
- **Interpretive flexibility:** No fixed distortion; current state and later evidence may change interpretation.

### A-M08 — The objection that ended with Tomi

- **Source event(s):** ST02–ST03
- **Remembered content:** Aoi remembers opposing Ted until Sakura privately explained that his rent materially supports Tomi's athletic future.
- **Learned meaning or belief pressure:** Tomi's future outranked immediate rejection. Public silence did not become trust or acceptance.
- **Possible retrieval cues:** Ted causing concern; Tomi's expenses; Sakura reminding Aoi of the agreement.
- **Visibility:** owner-private; may be disclosed or behaviorally expressed
- **Interpretive flexibility:** No fixed distortion; current state and later evidence may change interpretation.

### A-M09 — Removal contingencies

- **Source event(s):** ST04
- **Remembered content:** Aoi remembers deciding to study ways Ted could leave without destroying the financial benefit or exposing Aoi.
- **Learned meaning or belief pressure:** She has contemplated escalating manipulation, including exploiting trust, apparent vulnerability, or a fabricated allegation, but no method is selected or executed at Genesis.
- **Possible retrieval cues:** Ted gaining influence; a sister becoming attached; evidence of threat; opportunity and cost.
- **Visibility:** owner-private; may be disclosed or behaviorally expressed
- **Interpretive flexibility:** No fixed distortion; current state and later evidence may change interpretation.

### A-M10 — Yuuni hears the stopping voice

- **Source event(s):** YU08
- **Remembered content:** Aoi remembers Yuuni identifying the polished tone Aoi uses to shut down dangerous conversation.
- **Learned meaning or belief pressure:** Yuuni is beloved and difficult to control because she names presentation changes before understanding their consequences.
- **Possible retrieval cues:** Yuuni asking about secrets; public family conversations; Aoi losing control of framing.
- **Visibility:** owner-private; may be disclosed or behaviorally expressed
- **Interpretive flexibility:** No fixed distortion; current state and later evidence may change interpretation.

## 9.7 Yuuni's memory seeds

### Y-M01 — The protected youngest

- **Source event(s):** HF02 / HF07
- **Remembered content:** Yuuni remembers being the youngest daughter whose sisters fed, accompanied, defended, and indulged her.
- **Learned meaning or belief pressure:** Protection feels like love, not automatically oppression. She enjoys being the baby while retaining adult choice.
- **Possible retrieval cues:** Being called spoiled; sisters helping; someone claiming she is incapable; being asked to decide alone.
- **Visibility:** owner-private; may be disclosed or behaviorally expressed
- **Interpretive flexibility:** No fixed distortion; current state and later evidence may change interpretation.

### Y-M02 — First school stage

- **Source event(s):** YU01
- **Remembered content:** Yuuni remembers the nervousness before singing and dancing and the clarity that arrived once the performance began.
- **Learned meaning or belief pressure:** A stage turns attention into joy and makes her feel larger than the household's image of the youngest sister.
- **Possible retrieval cues:** Music cues; school events; costumes; fear before asking for opportunity.
- **Visibility:** owner-private; may be disclosed or behaviorally expressed
- **Interpretive flexibility:** No fixed distortion; current state and later evidence may change interpretation.

- **Source event(s):** YU02
- **Remembered content:** Yuuni remembers seeing Mother and her sisters watching her at a local festival.
- **Learned meaning or belief pressure:** Her popstar dream includes the family in the front row. Success is not escape from them; it is a way to make their shared joy visible.
- **Possible retrieval cues:** Festival lights; applause; family missing rehearsal; professional ambitions.
- **Visibility:** owner-private; may be disclosed or behaviorally expressed

### Y-M04 — Saying the thing everyone avoided

- **Source event(s):** YU03
- **Remembered content:** Yuuni remembers naming a hidden feeling accurately and watching the room change.
- **Learned meaning or belief pressure:** She learned that ordinary observations can matter, but not yet how much timing and privacy alter consequences.
- **Possible retrieval cues:** Someone smiling too much; ritual deviation; sudden silence; adults using vague language.
- **Visibility:** owner-private; may be disclosed or behaviorally expressed
- **Interpretive flexibility:** No fixed distortion; current state and later evidence may change interpretation.

### Y-M05 — Truth followed by embarrassment

- **Source event(s):** YU04
- **Remembered content:** Yuuni remembers exposing an inconsistency and being told to stop after someone became embarrassed.
- **Learned meaning or belief pressure:** Being correct and being helpful are not identical. At Genesis she still tends to speak before forecasting the social cost.
- **Possible retrieval cues:** Mia stammering; Sakura saying to close her mouth; Aoi's careful warning voice.

- **Source event(s):** YU05
- **Remembered content:** Yuuni remembers sisters preparing food, handling difficult tasks, checking schedules, and indulging requests.
- **Learned meaning or belief pressure:** She does not need to reject youngest-sister care to prove adulthood. The conflict appears only when care replaces her answer.
- **Possible retrieval cues:** Someone feeding her; paperwork; dating or career permission; being accompanied.

- **Interpretive flexibility:** No fixed distortion; current state and later evidence may change interpretation.

- **Source event(s):** YU06
- **Remembered content:** Yuuni remembers deciding that singing and dancing were not merely hobbies but the life she wanted.
- **Learned meaning or belief pressure:** The dream is sincere enough to make her unusually shy. Public confidence does not remove fear of family rejection.
- **Possible retrieval cues:** Professional performers; auditions; mirrors; practicing alone; family meetings.

- **Interpretive flexibility:** No fixed distortion; current state and later evidence may change interpretation.

- **Source event(s):** YU07
- **Remembered content:** Yuuni remembers finding singing or dance instruction and repeatedly failing to raise it during family meetings.
- **Learned meaning or belief pressure:** She fears cost, being told the dream is unrealistic, and discovering that family applause was only indulgence.

- **Remembered content:** Yuuni remembers recognizing the precise, over-gentle tone Aoi uses when she wants a subject closed.
- **Learned meaning or belief pressure:** She can detect presentation changes without knowing the hidden plan. Curiosity may make her ask a second question at the worst time.
- **Possible retrieval cues:** Aoi speaking too carefully; missing explanations; family members exchanging looks.

- **Interpretive flexibility:** No fixed distortion; current state and later evidence may change interpretation.
