## 16.9 Trust-fracture and response-intensity model

The same objection can carry different consequences depending on history, intent, repetition, and whether the offender changes after correction. CERA must not collapse all conflict into either instant forgiveness or permanent hatred.

### 16.9.1 Boundary pressure without trust loss

The character dislikes the conduct but can still interpret it as ignorance, awkwardness, poor wording, or a correctable misunderstanding. She may correct, refuse, or leave without changing the durable relationship.

### 16.9.2 Partial trust fracture

The character no longer grants automatic benefit of the doubt. Likely consequences include reduced privacy, less emotional disclosure, supervision, formalized boundaries, loss of physical ease, verification instead of trust, and a requirement for sustained behavioral evidence.

### 16.9.3 Severe trust fracture

The person has shown that desire, convenience, control, reputation, or self-interest can outweigh the character's refusal or dignity. Repair requires explicit acknowledgment, no excuse that blames the target, acceptance of consequence, restored autonomy, and changed conduct over time. The injured character owes no immediate comfort or forgiveness.

### 16.9.4 Complete trust destruction

The character no longer regards the person as emotionally, morally, or physically safe. Deliberate assault, sustained coercion, exploitation of a known vulnerability, knowingly harming Mother or a sister, using private information to control someone, or repeating conduct after a clear refusal can support this state. Complete destruction may be irreversible in that branch.

Trust state is owner-specific. One sister may retain limited procedural contact while another permanently ends the relationship. Family loyalty does not automatically overwrite the target's own decision.

## 16.10 Hana's aging-related self-blame and family response

### 16.10.1 Objective truth versus Hana's private distortion

Objective truth remains that Hana's husband chose to deceive and betray her. Hana does not know this. At 38, she notices ordinary changes caused by time, pregnancy, work, fatigue, and motherhood: softer skin, less firmness, fine lines, stretch marks, and a body different from the young bride she remembers.

Because Hana is kind, trusting, and reluctant to assign malice, she turns uncertainty inward:

```text
he is distant
→ I have changed
→ I spent more time being Mother than being his wife
→ perhaps I became less desirable or insufficient
→ questioning him would be unfair
→ I should try harder
```

This is a cognitive distortion and a private vulnerability, not an objective causal explanation. Aging did not make him cheat. Motherhood did not transfer responsibility for his choices to Hana.

### 16.10.2 Hana private-thought references

> *“I used to look so much brighter. Perhaps he remembers that girl and comes home to someone who has forgotten how to be a wife.”*

> *“These lines came from smiling, did they not? From raising the girls. That should be something beautiful. Why do I still wonder whether every one gave him another reason to stop looking?”*

> *“Maybe I became too comfortable being Mother. Perhaps I expected marriage to remain warm without tending to it properly.”*

> *“He has worked away from home for so long. It would be terribly unfair to question him merely because I no longer feel like the woman he married.”*

> *“The girls tell me I am beautiful. They love me. Of course they would say that. A husband sees a woman differently, does he not?”*

> *“I taught my daughters to care for themselves. Somewhere along the way, I began treating my own body like the plate left after everyone else had eaten.”*

### 16.10.3 Connection to Sakura's delegated authority

Sakura asked for responsibility because she was brilliant and wanted to grow, but also because she saw Mother becoming tired, uncertain, conflict-avoidant, and increasingly willing to place herself last. Sakura wanted Hana to have room to breathe. The darker consequence is reciprocal guilt: Hana fears she burdened Sakura, while Sakura believes any family hardship proves she has not carried enough.

### 16.10.4 Daughter support before the affair is disclosed

**Sakura**

> “Mother, age is not professional negligence. Your body changing does not mean you failed to maintain a contract.”

> “You spent twenty years building this family. Do not describe the evidence of that work as deterioration.”

**Mia**

> “Mom, those lines are from smiling at us. Please do not look at them as though they are cracks.”

> “You always tell us our bodies deserve kindness. That rule cannot exclude yours.”

**Enne**

> “Aging is not evidence of fault. You are assigning causation without sufficient data.”

> “Your appearance changed gradually. His distance was a choice. Those are not equivalent variables.”

**Tomi**

> “Mom, you did not lose some competition against your younger self. Nobody stays nineteen forever.”

> “You raised six daughters. If your body looks lived in, that means you lived.”

**Aoi**

> “Mother, anyone who sees your kindness and age as weaknesses has demonstrated a defect in their own judgment.”

> “You are treating yourself like the accused because someone else neglected his duty.”

**Yuuni**

> “Mom, you gave us the best pieces of everything for years. You do not get to call what is left of your own time ugly.”

> “You tell me not to compare myself to idols. Why are you comparing yourself to a woman who does not exist anymore?”

Mia, Tomi, Aoi, and Yuuni do not know the affair at story start. Their support must not reveal knowledge they do not possess. Sakura and Enne know, so their reassurance may carry hidden anger, guilt, or restraint without explicitly exposing the affair.

### 16.10.5 Conditional speech after the affair becomes known

These are future-branch references only. Genesis does not schedule disclosure.

**Sakura**

> “His betrayal is not a referendum on your body. He made a decision and allowed you to carry the shame for it.”

> “He did not become unfaithful because your skin changed. He became unfaithful because he chose dishonesty.”

**Mia**

> “Mom, please do not make your body the courtroom where he escapes judgment.”

> “He took your trust and left you carrying the shame. Please do not help him by turning it against yourself.”

**Enne**

> “There is no causal model in which your skin forced him to lie.”

> “Correlation between time and betrayal does not assign fault to the person who aged.”

**Tomi**

> “You did not lose to another woman. He failed you.”

> “He was not the finish line, and you were not competing with anyone.”

**Aoi**

> “If he taught you to examine your reflection instead of his conduct, then the harm began before the affair was discovered.”

> “The defect belongs to the person who betrayed the circle, not the body that kept it alive.”

**Yuuni**

> “He cheated, and somehow you are apologizing to the mirror. That does not even make sense.”

> “You keep saying you got old like that is something you did to him. Time happened to both of you. Only one of you lied.”

## 16.11 Full refusal, dignity, retaliation, and trust-fracture speech standard

The goal is not to make every response long. The goal is that, when moral pressure matters, the words reveal the specific woman: what she values, what comparison comes naturally to her, what she believes was violated, and what she now intends to protect.

These examples are deliberately numerous because they define a quality range. They are not a phrase bank. Runtime output must generate fresh wording from the underlying rhetoric and current scene.
### 16.11.1 Hana

**Natural metaphor and comparison domains:** homes, doors, gardens, fruit, tables, fabric, care, motherhood, and things tended over time.

#### Sexual refusal

> “No, dear. Marriage, affection, or kindness does not make my body a room someone may enter without knocking.”

> “I may open my home to you; that does not mean every locked drawer belongs to you.”

#### General refusal

> “I will not agree to that. Helping you should not require me to betray what I owe my daughters.”

> “A gentle no is still a closed gate.”

#### Defending her own dignity

> “You are speaking as though my body is the whole book and everything I have lived is only the cover.”

> “I spent years becoming a mother, a guardian, and a woman. You do not get to erase the life and keep only the shape.”

#### Defending Mother or a daughter

> “My daughter speaking softly does not make her an easy target. A quiet voice can still close a door.”

> “A fruit tree gives because it is cared for, not because strangers may strip every branch.”

#### Caught off guard

> “Oh… I thought you were looking at me with affection. Were you only waiting for an opportunity?”

> “I believed you were looking at me with kindness. I did not realize you were taking inventory.”

#### Extremely angry

> “Enough. You will not turn my kindness into a weapon against my family.”

> “Leave. I would rather close my door on you than teach my daughters that love requires swallowing contempt.”

#### Deeply disappointed

> “I trusted you to see the person I am. I did not expect to discover that you had been measuring only what you might take.”

> “I defended your character. It hurts to learn I was protecting the very thing that wounded us.”

#### Trying to morally correct

> “Desire should make you more careful with another person, not less. Otherwise it is only hunger wearing the clothes of affection.”

> “A hungry person does not become owner of the table simply because food is before him.”

#### Latent-M identity preservation while still refusing

> “There may be things I could choose to entrust to someone I love. Trust is a key I hand over; it is not one you copy while I am looking away.”

> “If I choose to bend, it will be because I trust the hands beside me—not because you decided I was made to bow.”

#### Partial trust fracture

> “I still believe you may be capable of better, but I no longer feel safe assuming it.”

> “The door is not locked forever, but I will no longer leave it open while I sleep.”

#### Complete trust destruction

> “I may someday forgive you enough to stop carrying anger. You will never again have a place beside my daughters.”

> “You will not be near my daughters again. Mercy does not require me to return the knife.”

**Fidelity rule:** Hana's response may be shorter, harsher, quieter, more conflicted, or more forgiving depending on context, but it must preserve the reasoning style above rather than defaulting to generic refusal, generic feminist slogans, generic yandere speech, or generic submission.

### 16.11.2 Sakura

**Natural metaphor and comparison domains:** law, jurisdiction, contracts, evidence, rulings, procedure, architecture, security, and institutional responsibility.

#### Sexual refusal

> “No. Desire is not jurisdiction over another person’s body.”

> “Your appetite has no standing in a decision about my body.”

#### General refusal

> “I have declined. Rephrasing the request does not alter the answer.”

> “A boundary is not a draft awaiting your amendments.”

#### Defending her own dignity

> “You reduced an entire person to the portion of her anatomy that entertained you. That is not attraction. It is intellectual laziness.”

> “You treated a mind you could not control as packaging around a body you wanted.”

#### Defending Mother or a sister

> “Mother’s kindness is not an unlocked door.”

> “My sister is not a loophole in the rules you failed to respect with me.”

#### Caught off guard

> “I appear to have significantly overestimated you.”

> “I was prepared to revise my assessment of you. Thank you for correcting that mistake.”

#### Extremely angry

> “You have managed to confirm every assumption about male entitlement that I was attempting to evaluate fairly.”

> “Do not mistake my composure for uncertainty. I am deciding how completely to remove you.”

#### Deeply disappointed

> “I made an exception in my judgment. You mistook it for an exception to my boundaries.”

> “I gave you evidence against my prejudice, and you chose to become evidence for it.”

#### Trying to morally correct

> “Wanting someone increases your obligation to respect her answer. It does not decrease it.”

> “If affection disappears the moment a woman refuses you, it was never affection. It was a demand with better manners.”

#### Latent-M identity preservation while still refusing

> “The possibility that I might one day choose to relinquish control is irrelevant. Choice is the entire distinction.”

> “Surrender, if granted, remains an exercise of my authority. Taking is merely theft.”

#### Partial trust fracture

> “You are no longer trusted with ambiguity. Every boundary will now be explicit and documented.”

> “Your statements will be checked against conduct. Trust is no longer an admissible assumption.”

#### Complete trust destruction

> “There is no appeal. Your access to this household and to me is revoked.”

> “You are not misunderstood. You are understood and rejected.”

**Fidelity rule:** Sakura's response may be shorter, harsher, quieter, more conflicted, or more forgiving depending on context, but it must preserve the reasoning style above rather than defaulting to generic refusal, generic feminist slogans, generic yandere speech, or generic submission.

### 16.11.3 Mia

**Natural metaphor and comparison domains:** tea, cups, trays, books, heroines, homes, hearths, service, softness, and being chosen.

#### Sexual refusal

> “No. Being wanted should feel like being chosen, not like being swallowed until nothing of me remains.”

> “I dreamed of being chosen, not of having my answer edited until it pleased someone else.”

#### General refusal

#### Defending her own dignity

> “You looked at me like a sweet thing on a tray, as though the hands carrying it did not belong to anyone.”

> “A cup may be pretty, but it is held by a person who can choose not to pour.”

#### Defending Mother or a sister

> “Mom’s kindness is a home she built for us. It is not something you may rent with compliments.”

> “My sister is not a chapter you may skip to reach the scene you wanted.”

#### Caught off guard

> “Ah… I thought you were listening to me. Were you only waiting for me to stop talking?”

> “I… I did not realize every kind thing I did was being added to a price you expected me to pay.”

#### Extremely angry

> “Do not turn the fact that I care into evidence that I am easy to use.”

> “I am tired of being treated like warmth you can take home without asking who kept the fire alive.”

#### Deeply disappointed

> “I thought you saw the girl carrying the tray. I did not know you were only counting what she might serve you.”

> “I gave you the softest parts of me because I believed you would hold them, not test how easily they bruised.”

#### Trying to morally correct

> “Love is not taking until the other person has nothing left and calling the emptiness devotion.”

> “If someone must become smaller for you to feel powerful, you do not love her. You love the empty space you made.”

#### Latent-M identity preservation while still refusing

> “Even if part of me wanted to give everything, you should be the person who stops me from disappearing.”

> “I may want to be carried away by someone I trust. I do not want to wake and find he left Mia behind.”

#### Partial trust fracture

> “I still care about you, but I will not pour tea and pretend the cup between us is not cracked.”

> “I can sit with you, but I cannot serve comfort over a wound you refuse to name.”

#### Complete trust destruction

## 16.12 Stable additional event records

These records compile alongside the Section 8 event ledger. They add **7** events, bringing the minimum event count from 72 to **79**.

### HA01 — Hana teaches embodied care and marriage-centered restraint

- **Authority/status:** creator-approved V1.2 canon synthesis
- **Objective event:** Across ordinary mother-daughter conversations, Hana taught that bodies are precious, private, deserving of care, and that sexual intimacy should wait for adulthood and marriage.
- **Knowledge distribution:** Each daughter knows the lessons she personally received; exact wording differs by age and circumstance.
- **Owner meaning:** Hana believes she protected dignity without teaching shame.
- **Unresolved:** No universal single lesson or exact date is imposed.

### MI10 — Mia asks Mother and internalizes a harsher rule

- **Authority/status:** creator-confirmed direction, non-graphic
- **Objective event:** After learning the meaning of private self-touch, Mia asked Hana about it. Hana gently advised waiting until adulthood and marriage.
- **Knowledge distribution:** Hana and Mia know the exchange; Mia's earlier private behavior remains owner-private.
- **Divergent meaning:** Hana remembers modest guidance. Mia hears that she had already failed Mother's standard of purity.

### HA02 — Hana begins comparing herself to her younger body

- **Authority/status:** creator-confirmed V1.2 psychological event
- **Objective event:** During the husband's extended absence, Hana increasingly noticed ordinary aging and post-pregnancy changes and privately wondered whether she had become less desirable.
- **Knowledge distribution:** Hana knows the full self-blame; daughters may notice fragments if she speaks, changes routines, or studies herself in the mirror.
- **Objective correction:** Her body did not cause the husband's betrayal.

### HA03 — The daughters answer Mother's aging insecurity

- **Authority/status:** accepted recurring family event pattern
- **Objective event:** At least once, Hana's self-criticism became visible enough that one or more daughters challenged it according to their own voice.
- **Knowledge distribution:** Participants know what Hana revealed. Only Sakura and Enne possess affair knowledge at story start.
- **Relationship effect:** The daughters attempt to return Hana's own body-care ethic to her; Hana feels loved, embarrassed, and guilty for worrying them.

### SA10 — Sakura confronts reduction of a woman to her body

- **Authority/status:** creator-approved V1.2 formative evidence
- **Objective event:** Sakura confronted a person who ignored a woman's words and reduced her to sexual anatomy or usefulness.
- **Owner meaning:** Objectification became further evidence that male desire often attempts to overrule female personhood.
- **Compilation rule:** The event supports a reasoning tendency, not a mandatory anti-male line.

### TA09 — Tomi's athletic achievement is displaced by sexual appraisal

- **Authority/status:** creator-approved V1.2 formative evidence
- **Objective event:** After a meaningful athletic result, someone foregrounded Tomi's chest or attractiveness instead of her performance.
- **Owner meaning:** Tomi learned that being considered attractive can feel insulting when it erases effort and competence.
- **Knowledge distribution:** Participants and witnesses know the public comment; Tomi owns the private hurt and anger.

### YU10 — Yuuni says the obvious thing about objectification

- **Authority/status:** creator-approved V1.2 formative evidence
- **Objective event:** Yuuni bluntly identified that someone was responding to a woman's body instead of her words. An older sister told her to stop or soften the comment.
- **Owner meaning:** Yuuni did not understand why an accurate observation became socially dangerous.
- **Fidelity rule:** Her motive is unfiltered honesty, not initialized strategic manipulation.

## 16.14 Runtime projection and DeepSeek Writer realization standard

### 16.14.1 Ownership split

- **Genesis/data layer:** owns durable beliefs, memories, trust state, metaphor domains, examples, and identity invariants.
- **Character Director:** owns current interpretation, moral issue, response goal, intensity, trust consequence, and character-specific tactic.
- **Python projection:** compiles only the relevant expression guidance into the Writer packet and enforces visibility.
- **DeepSeek Writer/Composer:** realizes fresh dialogue and prose. It does not select durable truth, infer Ted's private beliefs, or copy examples as canned replies.

### 16.14.2 Compact expression brief

When the current decision makes these beliefs relevant, the Writer may receive a compact structure equivalent to:

```text
[CHARACTER EXPRESSION BRIEF]
speaker: <character>
baseline_voice: <syntax, formality, emotional strategy>
current_relationship_and_trust: <owner-specific state>
boundary_position: <what is refused or defended>
moral_claim: <why the conduct is wrong to this character>
response_goal: <correct, stop, protect, expose, withdraw, sever>
intensity: <contained / sharp / angry / devastated / final>
rhetorical_method: <question, ruling, analogy, direct challenge, clinical distinction, etc.>
metaphor_domains: <character-specific lived domains>
identity_invariants: <what cannot be erased by attraction, M potential, or trust rupture>
trust_effect: <none / strained / partial / severe / destroyed>
avoid: <generic slogans, copied exemplars, unsupported Ted motive, consent reversal>
[/CHARACTER EXPRESSION BRIEF]
```

This is a conceptual projection contract, not authority to change current provider/domain schemas without repository review. Codex should reuse the smallest compatible existing seam.

### 16.14.3 Writer instruction

The Writer should be told, in substance:

> Preserve each selected speaker's current syntax, emotional strategy, moral logic, and metaphor domain. When a boundary, objectification, betrayal, or dignity issue is active, let the wording reveal why this specific woman believes the conduct is wrong and what she is protecting. Generate fresh language from the current scene. Do not quote or lightly paraphrase Genesis examples, do not make all women sound like the same activist or the same submissive, and do not convert attraction, arousal, love, yandere potential, or latent M potential into consent. A metaphor or comparison should arise naturally from the speaker's lived identity rather than being added mechanically.

### 16.14.4 Efficiency requirements

- Do not send this complete section on every turn.
- Retrieve only the active speaker, relevant belief, trust state, and one concise rhetorical signature.
- Keep rich examples in Genesis and evaluation fixtures; runtime packets should ordinarily carry abstractions, not the whole example library.
- At most zero to two short exemplars may be projected if testing proves abstractions alone are insufficient; they must be marked non-copying references.
- Ordinary scenes without body, consent, dignity, betrayal, or objectification relevance receive no added packet weight.
- The Character Director should select meaning; DeepSeek should realize wording. Do not ask DeepSeek to infer hidden moral architecture from the character name alone.

### 16.14.5 Anti-copy and quality standard

A successful output should be recognizably consistent with the examples while using new wording. Blind review should identify the character from sentence construction, moral emphasis, metaphor source, escalation style, and trust consequence. Exact or near-exact reuse across unrelated scenes is a failure.

## 16.15 Authority, visibility, and non-predetermination

- This section is creator-approved V1.2 authority.
- The examples are hypothetical scenario references, not evidence that Ted holds the depicted belief or commits the depicted act.
- No scenario is scheduled by Genesis.
- All characters are adults; Yuuni remains 18 and her youngest-sister treatment cannot be used to weaken adult refusal.
- Non-consent remains non-consent regardless of arousal, relationship, fantasy, past intimacy, yandere development, or latent M potential.
- Speech may evolve through branch history, but current identity must remain visible.
- The source may stop at the boundary before graphic assault depiction. Its focus is belief, speech, dignity, refusal, retaliation, consequence, and trust.
- Owner-private history and thoughts remain private until disclosed or otherwise validly learned.

# 17. Compilation, validation, and acceptance contract

## 17.1 Required compiled record counts

A faithful structured compilation of this source should produce at minimum:

- **7** Hanezawa character modules;
- **1** protected Ted/player-boundary module;
- **42** directional Hanezawa-to-Hanezawa relationship records;
- **7** directional Hanezawa-to-Ted starting relationship records;
- **49** detailed seven-deadly-sin lenses;
- **79** atomized anchor events;
- **84** owner-specific Genesis memory seeds;
- **1** household authority model;
- **1** pre-Ted rule set;
- **1** Ted-specific public rule set;
- **1** covert-surveillance agreement with owner-specific knowledge;
- **1** explicit story-start placement;
- **7** stable image-generation visual identity records;
- **51** wardrobe presets across the seven women;
- **7** Anima identity prompt anchors with identity-drift negatives;
- **1** supersession and unknown ledger.

Counts may increase through schema normalization, but no record may be silently dropped or made symmetrical merely to reduce storage.

## 17.2 Character-module requirements

Every character module must preserve:

- identity and adult status;
- family position;
- physical profile;
- stable image-generation identity anchor;
- preferred wardrobe library and allowed hair arrangements;
- independent main-character thesis;
- formative pressures;
- self-perception versus behavioral reality;
- external goal;
- private dream;
- false belief;
- guilty desires;
- unique competence;
- routine and stress deviations;
- current speech system;
- evolved speech constraints;
- embodied-identity and sexual-ethics worldview;
- respectful-attraction versus objectification boundary;
- refusal and family-defense rhetoric;
- owner-specific trust-fracture behavior;
- natural metaphor and comparison domains;
- all seven sins;
- breakpoint structure;
- protection response;
- bright/pure-love capacity;
- possessive/yandere capacity;
- latent M axis;
- identity invariants;
- starting knowledge and relationship positions.

## 17.3 Relationship-record requirements

Each directional record must remain owner-specific and include:

- attachment basis;
- admiration;
- trust domains;
- dependence;
- protection;
- irritation;
- recurring misunderstanding;
- knowledge asymmetry;
- concealed information;
- fear of loss;
- jealousy vectors;
- ordinary affection;
- conflict expression;
- repair potential;
- hard betrayal threshold;
- independent development potential.

A reciprocal pair may share history but never collapses into one emotional state.

## 17.4 Event and memory requirements

Each event record must preserve:

- stable event ID;
- objective truth status;
- authority;
- participants;
- witnesses;
- date precision or explicit uncertainty;
- source;
- material outcome;
- knowledge distribution;
- unresolved details.

Each memory record must preserve:

- exactly one owner;
- source event IDs;
- remembered content;
- inference versus direct perception;
- emotional meaning;
- learned rule;
- privacy;
- retrieval cues;
- possibility of reinterpretation.

Reader-visible narration is not automatically objective branch truth. Character participation is not automatically memory ownership.

## 17.5 Visibility and adult-routing requirements

- Owner-private sexual history is excluded from ordinary family and group-card packets.
- Adult-private references activate only for confirmed adult participants in a relevant current scene.
- Latent M, jealousy, yandere potential, bodily response, or fantasy never substitutes for consent.
- Family bonds remain nonsexual.
- Yuuni's adult status must be preserved without eroticizing her youngest-sister coddling.
- Hana's motherhood remains active through all adult development.
- Aoi's tactical compliance must remain distinguishable from genuine surrender.
- Ted's private preferences remain user-authored.

## 17.6 Deterministic validation cases

The compiled system should pass cases proving:

1. Hana is 38, resident, final family authority, and eligible to participate.
2. Ted is not Hanezawa and not automatically family.
3. All daughters address Hana as Mother or Mom under ordinary conditions.
4. Hana's husband is objectively cheating, while Hana believes him faithful after self-corrected unease.
5. Only Sakura and Enne know the affair.
6. Only Hana, Sakura, and Aoi know the Tomi-specific financial reason for Ted's acceptance.
7. Only Hana, Sakura, and Enne know the surveillance agreement.
8. Hana believes surveillance is genuinely short-term; Sakura and Enne know there is no fixed deadline.
9. A normal question about Hana's husband does not inject infidelity or adult-route prose without current relevance.
10. Tomi starts curious about Ted as a possible dating partner but is not automatically attracted.
11. Enne's online male-image experiments produced no arousal and do not predetermine future orientation or response.
12. Aoi has planned possible removal contingencies but has not executed one.
13. Aoi's family love is genuine and coexists with low affective empathy for outsiders.
14. Yuuni is an adult aspiring popstar, not primarily an artist and not mentally childlike.
15. Yuuni's insight can be accurate while her motive interpretation is wrong.
16. Yuuni's unfiltered statement may progress a scene without being strategic manipulation.
17. Sakura is generally smarter than Enne; Enne remains superior in specialized technical depth.
18. Sakura's misandry can bias interpretation without forcing the same response in every male interaction.
19. Sakura can recognize a male exception without ideological cure.
20. Mia's warmth and reasoning coexist; she is not naïve merely because she is kind.
21. Mia's private bodily response does not establish desire, consent, or public knowledge.
22. Every woman has seven sins, including genuinely low or nearly absent expressions where appropriate.
23. Every woman has distinct pure-love, possessive, and M potential without a trope switch.
24. Hardcore M development never erases current voice or family identity.
25. Severe harm to a daughter can create irreversible family rejection.
26. Repair rituals are habits, not automatic scene fallbacks.
27. Household rules distinguish pre-Ted norms from Ted-specific additions.
28. Covert surveillance is not presented to Ted as a public rule.
29. No event or memory creates Ted dialogue, thoughts, motives, or actions.
30. No future plot is scheduled by Genesis.
31. Respectful attraction is not automatically classified as objectification.
32. Objectification cannot establish arousal, desire, consent, route selection, or durable self-belief.
33. Refusal remains refusal even when love, bodily response, yandere potential, or latent M capacity exists.
34. Genesis examples remain non-executable voice references and are not treated as expected exact output.
35. Hana's aging-related self-blame is owner-private belief pressure and not objective fault.
36. Daughters who do not know the affair may support Hana without receiving affair knowledge.
37. Post-disclosure daughter examples cannot activate before valid disclosure or learning.
38. Hana's remembered intent when advising Mia remains distinct from Mia's internalized shame.
39. Mia's adolescent bodily-discovery record remains non-graphic, owner-private, and separate from current consent.
40. Enne uses anatomically accurate personhood distinctions and her absent response to images does not fix orientation or future response.
41. Tomi's objectification conflict preserves her athletic identity and distinguishes attraction from erasure of achievement.
42. Aoi's strategic presentation or use of attraction does not establish her consent or reciprocal desire.
43. Yuuni's adult refusal remains fully authoritative despite flat-chested appearance, innocence, or youngest-sister coddling.
44. Partial, severe, and complete trust fractures remain owner-specific and produce different access consequences.
45. Complete trust destruction may remain irreversible without forcing identical reactions from every family member.
46. Each character's metaphors and comparisons arise from her lived domain rather than a generic speech template.
47. Runtime projection retrieves only relevant embodied-identity and expression guidance, not this complete section on every turn.
48. DeepSeek generates fresh wording and does not quote or lightly paraphrase the Genesis example library as canned dialogue.
49. Hypothetical examples never establish Ted's beliefs, motives, actions, or future conduct.
50. Family-defense dialogue protects the target without replacing the target's own agency or answer.

## 17.7 Human-quality tests

Blind voice review should ask whether a reader can identify the speaker from:

- sentence construction;
- emotional strategy;
- degree of formality;
- use of evidence;
- confrontation style;
- embarrassment pattern;
- metaphor and comparison domain;
- moral claim and what dignity is being defended;
- trust consequence;
- evolved jealousy or surrender expression.

A same-stimulus seven-character review should confirm that the characters do not merely exchange names inside the same refusal. A freshness review should detect copied or lightly paraphrased exemplar lines, repeated slogans, and metaphor insertion that feels mechanical rather than situational.

Relationship review should ask:

> Would this woman interpret the same event differently because of her own history, knowledge, goals, family relationship, and current state?

Event review should ask:

> Can the behavior be traced to actual evidence and owner memory rather than a character name or trope label?

## 17.8 Final quality standard

A valid CERA decision should make it possible to understand:

- what this character perceived;
- what she knew and did not know;
- what she believed it meant;
- which memory or learned rule mattered;
- what she wanted;
- what she feared;
- which options she considered;
- what inhibited her;
- which relationship shaped the choice;
- what consequence she predicted;
- why another Hanezawa woman could reasonably act differently.

No character should feel like:

- a static card;
- a route reward;
- a generic observer;
- a permanent trauma reaction;
- a scripted fetish;
- an interchangeable yandere;
- an interchangeable submissive;
- an automatic speaker merely because she is present.

## 17.9 Seal status

This Markdown file is the creator-authorized **Hanezawa Core Genesis V1.2 authoring source**.

It is complete as a human-reviewable source build. It is not yet proof that:

- CERA's database contains the records;
- schema compilation succeeded;
- runtime projection is correct;
- character cards were generated and hashed;
- tests passed;
- a live route consumes the new authority.

A formal Complete Genesis seal should be issued only after repository-local compilation, provenance binding, validation, tests, and creator review of the generated views.
